(2) ନିଜ ଶିଷ୍ୟର ଗୁରୁ ପ୍ରତି ଶ୍ରଦ୍ଧା ଦେଖି
(3) ଶିଷ୍ୟର ଗୁରୁଙ୍କ ପ୍ରତି ବିଶ୍ୱାସକୁ ଦେଖି
(4) ଶିଷ୍ୟ ଉଚ୍ଛୃଙ୍ଖଳ ହେବା କାରଣରୁ
= 1
~ A teacher should feel proud on seeing the pupil's skill and proficiency — option A, as per OPSC's key. The passage first says that Eklavya, though an outsider (ଅସବର୍ଣ୍ଣ, ଅପାଂକ୍ତେୟ), showed Drona how a learner can master knowledge and cross every traditional barrier, and then adds 'ଯେକୌଣସି ଆଚାର୍ଯ୍ୟ ଏଥିରେ ଗୌରବାନ୍ୱିତ ଅନୁଭବ କରିବାର କଥା' — any teacher ought to feel proud of this. 'This' (ଏଥିରେ) is the display of the pupil's learning. Why not the others: (B) and (C) are traps built from the words ଶ୍ରଦ୍ଧା and ବିଶ୍ୱାସ in the passage, but there they are the means by which Eklavya crossed the barriers; what earns the teacher's pride is the result — the skill he gained; (D) indiscipline (ଉଚ୍ଛୃଙ୍ଖଳତା) would make a teacher angry, not proud. The writer's point is that a true teacher rejoices when a student excels, even one he had turned away — he should even be ready to learn from that pupil. Word help: ଗୌରବାନ୍ୱିତ = filled with pride; ପରଂପରାଗତ ନିୟନ୍ତ୍ରଣ = traditional restrictions; ଲଂଘନ = crossing, overstepping. Tip: in passage questions, trace a pronoun like ଏଥିରେ back to the idea it stands for.

Q 198 | odi | Literature & Comprehension
(Passage as in Q196.)
ଆଚାର୍ଯ୍ୟ ଦ୍ରୋଣଙ୍କଠାରେ ନିଜଶିଷ୍ୟ ପ୍ରତି କେଉଁ ଗୁଣ ପ୍ରକାଶ ପାଇଛି ?
(1) ଶ୍ରଦ୍ଧାଶୀଳ
(2) ଇର୍ଷାତୁର
(3) ନିଷ୍କପଟ
(4) ଦୟାଭାବ
= 2
~ Drona showed envy (ଇର୍ଷାତୁର) towards his pupil — option B. The passage never uses the word 'envy', but it is clear from his conduct: a true teacher would have felt proud of Eklavya's skill and embraced him; instead Drona came down from the seat of a teacher (ଆଚାର୍ଯ୍ୟର ଆସନରୁ ତଳକୁ ଓହ୍ଲାଇ) so that Eklavya could be punished for his 'audacity'. In the Mahabharata he asks for Eklavya's right thumb as gurudakshina, partly to keep his promise that no archer would surpass his favourite pupil Arjuna. The writer sums up: lineage and high birth (କୌଳିକତା, କୁଳୀନତା) won, but the office of the teacher was insulted. Why not the others: (A) reverence (ଶ୍ରଦ୍ଧାଶୀଳ) was Eklavya's quality, not Drona's; (C) guileless or sincere (ନିଷ୍କପଟ) does not fit a teacher who used gurudakshina to disable a pupil; (D) kindness (ଦୟାଭାବ) is the very thing missing. Word help: ଇର୍ଷାତୁର = consumed by envy; ନିଷ୍କପଟ = without deceit. Tip: 'which quality is shown' questions often need inference — judge by what the character does, not only by the words used.

Q 199 | odi | Literature & Comprehension
(Passage as in Q196.)
ଦ୍ରୋଣାଚାର୍ଯ୍ୟଙ୍କ ଭିତରେ କେତେବେଳେ ଅସଲ ଗୁରୁ ନବଜନ୍ମ ଲାଭ କରିଥାଆନ୍ତା ?
(1) ଏକଲବ୍ୟ ପ୍ରତିଭାକୁ ଦେଖି ତାକୁ କୁଣ୍ଢାଇ ଧରିଥିଲେ
(2) ଏକଲବ୍ୟକୁ ଇର୍ଷା କରିଥିଲେ
(3) ତାଙ୍କଠାରୁ ଏକଲବ୍ୟ ବିଦ୍ୟା ଶିକ୍ଷା କରିଥିଲେ
(4) ଦ୍ରୋଣାଚାର୍ଯ୍ୟଙ୍କୁ ଏକଲବ୍ୟ ପ୍ରଶଂସା କରିଥିଲେ
= 1
~ The real guru inside Drona would have been reborn if he had embraced Eklavya on seeing his talent — option A. The passage puts it as a chain of 'would have' statements: had Drona's heart been a true teacher's heart, he would have embraced Eklavya that day (କୁଣ୍ଢାଇ ଧରିଥାନ୍ତେ); the guru would have learned from the pupil; the slough (ନିର୍ମୋକ) of tradition would have fallen away; and the real guru in him would have been born anew (ନବଜନ୍ମ ଲାଭ କରିଥାଆନ୍ତା). Why not the others: (B) envy is what actually blocked that rebirth, so it is the opposite; (C) reverses the passage — the writer says the guru would have learned from the student, not the student from the guru; (D) Eklavya praising Drona is not mentioned. Grammar note: verb forms like ଧରିଥାନ୍ତେ, ଶିଖିଥାନ୍ତା and ଲାଭ କରିଥାଆନ୍ତା express a condition that was never fulfilled, so the passage is describing what should have happened, not what did. Word help: ନବଜନ୍ମ = rebirth; ନିର୍ମୋକ = the cast-off skin of a snake, used here for an outer shell of custom. Tip: in 'when would X have happened' questions, look for the ଯଦି/ହୋଇଥିଲେ clause in the passage.

Q 200 | odi | Literature & Comprehension
(Passage as in Q196.)
ଏକଲବ୍ୟର ସାହସକୁ କିଏ ଦୁଃସାହସ ବୋଲି ମନେ କରିଛନ୍ତି?
(1) ଧୃତରାଷ୍ଟ୍ର
(2) ବ୍ୟାସଦେବ
(3) ଦ୍ରୋଣାଚାର୍ଯ୍ୟ
(4) ଆଚାର୍ଯ୍ୟ
= 2
~ Vyasadeva, the author of the Mahabharata, is the one the writer says saw Eklavya's courage as audacity (ଦୁଃସାହସ) — option B. The passage argues that Vyasa's poetic vision perhaps lacked the courage to place human (even spiritual) good above social convention; 'ତେଣୁ ଏକଲବ୍ୟର ସାହସ ତାଙ୍କୁ ପ୍ରଧାନତଃ ଦୁଃସାହସ ବୋଲି ହିଁ ମନେହେଲା', and so he looked for a 'literary justice' to punish it. To make that punishment look necessary, Drona had to be brought down from the teacher's seat. Why not the others: (A) Dhritarashtra does not appear in the passage at all; (C) Drona is the trap — he carries out the punishment inside the story, but the writer blames the poet who shaped the story; (D) 'ଆଚାର୍ଯ୍ୟ' is just a general word for teacher, not a person named as holding this view. Context: Veda Vyasa (Krishna Dvaipayana) is the traditional author of the Sanskrit Mahabharata; in Odisha, Sarala Das retold it in Odia as the Sarala Mahabharata in the 15th century. Word help: ଦୁଃସାହସ = rash daring; ପ୍ରେୟ = what is pleasing, ଶ୍ରେୟ = what is truly good; ସାହିତ୍ୟିକ ନ୍ୟାୟ = poetic justice. Tip: separate the author of a work from the characters inside it.
